**Final Verdict:** The solution contains a **Critical Error** and is therefore **invalid**.

---

### List of Findings
* **Location:** “Indeed, $b-a\ge 0$ because $a,b\ge 1$ …”  
  **Issue:** **Critical Error** – the claim that $b-a\ge 0$ for all points $(a,b)\in S_n$ is false; points with $b<a$ (e.g. $(2,1)$) belong to $S_n$ and have $b-a<0$. This mistake invalidates the subsequent coverage argument and the construction does not actually cover all required points for many values of $k$.

* **Location:** The whole “Verification that every point of $S_n$ is covered” (cases 1–3, especially the derivation of inequality (4) and the use of (5)).  
  **Issue:** **Critical Error** – the argument assumes $t=b-a\ge 0$ and treats only the case $t\ge k$. Points with negative $t$ are ignored, and the derived bound $a\le n-k$ does not hold for them. Consequently, for many admissible $(a,b)$ the construction provides no line containing the point.

---

## Detailed Verification Log

Below the solution is reproduced step‑by‑step, each step is examined, and any problems are highlighted.

---

### Step 1 – Notation  

> “A line is sunny iff its slope is different from $0$ (horizontal), $\infty$ (vertical) and $-1$ (the line $x+y=0$).  
> Let $S_n=\{(a,b)\in\mathbb Z_{>0}^2\mid a+b\le n+1\}$.”

*Correct.* The definition of “sunny” matches the problem statement, and $S_n$ is precisely the set of points that must be covered.

---

### Step 2 – Key observation  

> “For any integer $t$ the line $\ell_t:\; y=x+t$ has slope $1$ and therefore is sunny.  
> A point $(a,b)\in S_n$ satisfies $b-a=t$ precisely when it lies on $\ell_t$.

> The possible values of the difference $b-a$ for points of $S_n$ are the integers $0,1,\dots ,n-1$.
> Indeed, $b-a\ge 0$ because $a,b\ge 1$, and from $a+b\le n+1$ we obtain $b-a\le n-1$.”

*Problem.*  
The claim **“$b-a\ge 0$ because $a,b\ge 1$”** is false.  
For example, $(a,b)=(2,1)$ belongs to $S_n$ (when $n\ge 3$) but $b-a=-1<0$.  
Hence the set of possible differences $b-a$ is **not** limited to $\{0,1,\dots ,n-1\}$; it also includes negative integers down to $-(n-2)$.  
Because this statement is used to restrict the family of sunny lines to those with non‑negative intercepts, the whole construction that follows is built on a false premise.

*Impact.* This error propagates to the coverage proof; any point with $b<a$ will not be accounted for by the sunny lines $\ell_t$ with $t\ge 0$.

---

### Step 3 – Construction for a given $k$  

> “Sunny part. Take the $k$ sunny lines $\ell_t$ $(t=0,1,\dots ,k-1)$, i.e. $y=x+t$.  
> Non‑sunny part. For the remaining $n-k$ lines we use the vertical lines $x=j$ $(j=1,2,\dots ,n-k)$.”

*Correct* in the sense that the lines are distinct and have the claimed slopes.  
However, because of the error in Step 2, the chosen sunny lines **do not** cover all points whose difference $b-a$ is negative, and the vertical lines are only placed at $x=1,\dots ,n-k$. For many points with $a>n-k$ and $b<a$, no line from the construction passes through them (see the counter‑example below). Thus the construction does **not** satisfy the covering requirement for many admissible $k$.

---

### Step 4 – Verification that every point of $S_n$ is covered  

> “Let $(a,b)\in S_n$ and put $t=b-a$. By (1) we have $0\le t\le n-1$.

> *Case 1:* $t\le k-1$ … (covered by $\ell_t$).  
> *Case 2:* $t\ge k$ … (derive $a\le (n+1-t)/2\le (n+1-k)/2\le n-k$, then use vertical line $x=a$).  
> *Case 3:* $k=n$ … (all points covered by sunny lines).”

*Critical Errors.*

1. **Assumption $0\le t\le n-1$** is false (see Step 2). Points with $t<0$ are omitted from the analysis.

2. **Case 2 analysis** assumes $t\ge k\ge 0$, so $t$ is non‑negative. It never addresses the situation $t<0$, which is precisely where the construction may fail.

3. The inequality chain leading to $a\le n-k$ is valid only for non‑negative $t$. For a point with $t<0$, the derived bound does **not** hold, and the argument that “$x=a$ is one of the vertical lines” breaks down when $a>n-k$.

**Concrete counter‑example.**  
Take $n=5$, $k=2$. The construction gives sunny lines $y=x$ and $y=x+1$, and vertical lines $x=1,2,3$.  
Consider the point $(a,b)=(4,1)$.  
- $a+b=5\le 6$, so $(4,1)\in S_5$.  
- $t=b-a=-3<0$, so it is not on any $\ell_t$ with $t\ge0$.  
- The vertical line $x=4$ is **not** among the chosen vertical lines (only $x=1,2,3$).  
Thus $(4,1)$ is uncovered, contradicting the claim that all points are covered.

Because the covering argument fails for many $(a,b)$, the construction does **not** prove existence of configurations for the claimed values of $k$.

---

### Step 5 – Counting sunny lines  

> “The lines in (2) have slope $1$ and are therefore sunny; the lines in (3) are vertical and not sunny. Hence the total number of sunny lines … is exactly $k$.”

*Correct* as a counting statement, but it is irrelevant because the configuration itself does not satisfy the covering condition.

---

### Step 6 – Impossibility of larger $k$  

> “Because we are allowed only $n$ distinct lines, the number of sunny lines cannot exceed $n$.”

*Correct* (trivial upper bound). However, the lower bound (that every $k$ with $0\le k\le n$ is attainable) is not established due to the critical error above.

---

### Conclusion  

The solution’s central construction relies on the false claim that $b-a\ge 0$ for all points in $S_n$. Consequently, the covering proof omits a whole class of points and the construction fails for many values of $k$ (e.g., $n=5$, $k=2$). This is a **Critical Error** that invalidates the entire argument. Therefore the solution is not correct.
